API Gateway Entry Point - Scan Request Initiation\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/services/api_gateway/routes.cpp\n// Lines: 209-239\n\n/**\n * POST /v1/security/scan\n */\nvoid initiateScan(const Http::Request& req,\n                  Http::ResponseWriter resp,\n                  const std::string& corrId)\n{\n    const auto tenantId = ensureAuthenticated(req);\n\n    rateLimiter_.acquireToken(tenantId, \"security.scan\");\n\n    json payload = parseJson(req);\n    validators::RequestValidator::validateScanRequest(payload);\n\n    auto dispatchResult = bus_->publish(\"InitiateSecurityScan\", {\n        { \"correlationId\", corrId },\n        { \"tenantId\",      tenantId },\n        { \"scanDepth\",     payload.at(\"scanDepth\") },\n        { \"targets\",       payload.at(\"targets\") }\n    });\n\n    if (!dispatchResult) {\n        throw errors::HttpError(Http::Code::Service_Unavailable,\n                                \"Unable to queue security scan at this time\");\n    }\n\n    resp.send(Http::Code::Accepted, json{\n        { \"message\", \"Security scan queued\" },\n        { \"correlationId\", corrId }\n    }.dump());\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Command Handler Orchestration Layer\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/lib/orchestration/command_handler.cpp\n// Lines: 178-217\n\nclass CommandHandler final\n{\npublic:\n    explicit CommandHandler(std::shared_ptr<CommandRegistry> registry)\n        : registry_{ std::move(registry) }\n    {\n        if (!registry_) { throw std::invalid_argument(\"registry is nullptr\"); }\n    }\n\n    // Synchronous (blocking) processing\n    void handle(const CommandContext& ctx, const Command& cmd)\n    {\n        notify_start(ctx, cmd);\n        try\n        {\n            run_validation(ctx, cmd);\n            run_execution(ctx, cmd);\n            notify_success(ctx, cmd);\n        }\n        catch (const std::exception& ex)\n        {\n            notify_error(ctx, cmd, ex);\n            throw;      // Re-propagate to upstream orchestrator\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Asynchronous (detached) processing — returns a future\n    std::future<void> dispatch_async(CommandContext ctx, Command cmd)\n    {\n        return std::async(std::launch::async,\n                          [self = shared_from_this(), ctx = std::move(ctx), cmd = std::move(cmd)]() {\n                              self->handle(ctx, cmd);\n                          });\n    }\n```\n\n### 3. **CRITICAL BOTTLENECK**: Scanner Engine with Single FIFO Queue\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/services/scanner_svc/scanner_engine.cpp\n// Lines: 200-241\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Thread-safe Queue (SIMPLE FIFO - NO PRIORITY)\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\ntemplate <typename T>\nclass BlockingQueue\n{\npublic:\n    explicit BlockingQueue(std::size_t max) : capacity_(max) {}\n\n    void push(T item)\n    {\n        std::unique_lock lk(mu_);\n        cvFull_.wait(lk, [&] { return queue_.size() < capacity_; });\n        queue_.push(std::move(item));  // FIFO: items processed in arrival order\n        cvEmpty_.notify_one();\n    }\n\n    bool pop(T &out)\n    {\n        std::unique_lock lk(mu_);\n        cvEmpty_.wait(lk, [&] { return !queue_.empty() || terminated_; });\n        if (queue_.empty()) return false; // terminated\n        out = std::move(queue_.front());\n        queue_.pop();\n        cvFull_.notify_one();\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    // ...\nprivate:\n    std::size_t                capacity_;\n    std::queue<T>              queue_;  // std::queue = FIFO, NO PRIORITY\n    std::mutex                 mu_;\n    std::condition_variable    cvFull_, cvEmpty_;\n    bool                       terminated_ {false};\n};\n```\n\n### 4. Scanner Engine Worker Pool - Shared Across All Tenants\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/services/scanner_svc/scanner_engine.cpp\n// Lines: 246-274\n\nclass ScannerEngine\n{\npublic:\n    explicit ScannerEngine(std::size_t workerCount = std::thread::hardware_concurrency())\n        : queue_(1024), log_(), shuttingDown_(false)  // Single queue with fixed capacity\n    {\n        workerCount = std::max<std::size_t>(1, workerCount);\n        log_.log(LogLevel::kInfo, \"Initializing ScannerEngine with \", workerCount, \" workers.\");\n\n        for (std::size_t i = 0; i < workerCount; ++i)\n        {\n            workers_.emplace_back([this, i] { workerLoop(i); });  // All workers share ONE queue\n        }\n    }\n\n    void submit(const ScanCommand &cmd)\n    {\n        if (shuttingDown_.load()) throw std::runtime_error(\"Engine is shutting down\");\n        queue_.push(cmd);  // ALL scan commands go to the SAME queue regardless of tenant type\n    }\n\n    // ...\nprivate:\n    BlockingQueue<ScanCommand>                  queue_;  // SINGLE shared queue for ALL tenants\n    std::vector<std::thread>                    workers_;\n    // ...\n};\n```\n\n### 5. Pay-As-You-Go Strategy - Has Its Own Priority Queue (But Not Used at Engine Level)\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/lib/domain/strategies/payg_scan_strategy.h\n// Lines: 220-236\n\nstruct QueueItem\n{\n    std::string correlationId;\n    ScanRequest request;\n\n    /* Highest priority first – normal > high => comparator reversed. */\n    bool operator<(const QueueItem& rhs) const noexcept\n    {\n        return static_cast<int>(request.priority) <\n               static_cast<int>(rhs.request.priority);\n    }\n};\n\n// ...\n/* Pending queue (priority). */\nstd::priority_queue<QueueItem> pending_;  // Strategy has priority queue internally\n```\n\n### 6. Continuous Scan Strategy - Background Worker Thread Generates Constant Load\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/lib/domain/strategies/continuous_scan_strategy.h\n// Lines: 186-199\n\n// Internal run-loop --------------------------------------------------------\nvoid runLoop()\n{\n    while (running_)\n    {\n        const auto now = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();\n        executeOneScanSafely();  // Generates continuous scan requests\n\n        std::unique_lock<std::mutex> lk(cvMutex_);\n        // Wait until either stop() is invoked or the cadence elapses.\n        cv_.wait_until(lk, now + cadence_, [this] { return !running_; });\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 7. Event Flow Documentation - Shows All Events Go Through Same Bus\n\n```markdown\n// File: /app/project/docs/architecture/event_flows.md\n// Lines: 13-38\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    autonumber\n    actor Client as Tenant / API Consumer\n    participant API as Presentation & API\n    participant ORCH as Orchestration\n    participant BUS as Event-Bus\n    participant SCN as ScannerSvc (Domain)\n\n    Client->>API: POST /v1/scan\\n{asset: \"web-1\", tier:\"deep\"}\n    API->>ORCH: Cmd<InitiateSecurityScan>\n    ORCH->>BUS: Publish(Cmd<InitiateSecurityScan>)\n    %% Broadcast to interested domain services\n    BUS-->>SCN: Cmd<InitiateSecurityScan>\n    SCN->>SCN: Validate + Enrich\n    // ALL scan commands go to the SAME ScannerSvc regardless of tenant type\n```\n\n### 8. ADR-002 - Event-Driven Architecture with Multi-Tenant QoS Note\n\n```markdown\n// File: /app/project/docs/architecture/adr/002-event-driven-architecture.md\n// Lines: 58-61\n\n### Follow-ups\n* Implement **Schema Registry** (Apache Avro) + compatibility gates in CI.\n* Define **tenant partition-key strategy** for QoS isolation.  // <-- THIS WAS NEVER IMPLEMENTED\n* Provide **SDKs** (Go, Rust, C++) with consistent observability hooks.\n```\n\n---\n\n## Analysis\n\n### 1. End-to-End Request Lifecycle for On-Demand Security Scans\n\nThe lifecycle of an on-demand security scan request follows this path:\n\n```\n[Client]\n    → POST /v1/security/scan (HTTP/REST)\n    → [API Gateway] routes.cpp:initiateScan()\n        → Authentication via JWT/mTLS\n        → Rate limiting per tenant\n        → JSON validation\n    → [Event Bus] Publish \"InitiateSecurityScan\" event\n    → [Orchestration Layer] command_handler.cpp\n        → Chain-of-Responsibility validation (TenantQuotaValidator, ComplianceValidator)\n        → Strategy resolution based on command type\n    → [Event Consumer] event_consumer.cpp\n        → Kafka consumer dispatches to domain handlers\n    → [Scanner Service] service_impl.cpp:InitiateScan()\n        → Acquires scan strategy from registry\n        → Publishes ScanStarted domain event\n        → Enqueues work to ScannerEngine\n    → [Scanner Engine] scanner_engine.cpp\n        → Work item placed in BlockingQueue\n        → Worker thread picks up and executes scan\n    → [Event Bus] Publish scan completion event\n```\n\n### 2. Architectural Interactions Between Tenant Types\n\nThe system supports different subscription plans with distinct scan behaviors:\n\n**Continuous Scan Tenants:**\n- Have a `ContinuousScanStrategy` that runs a background thread\n- Generates scan requests at a configurable cadence (minimum 1 second)\n- Produces a **constant stream** of scan commands regardless of user activity\n- These scans are typically automated, running 24/7\n\n**Pay-As-You-Go (PAYG) Tenants:**\n- Use `PayGScanStrategy` triggered by explicit user requests\n- On-demand, sporadic scan requests\n- Users expect immediate responsiveness\n- Billed per-scan, so latency directly impacts customer experience\n\n**The Critical Interaction:**\nBoth tenant types' scan requests ultimately flow through the **same ScannerEngine** with its **single shared FIFO BlockingQueue**. The architectural separation at the Strategy level does NOT extend to the execution layer.\n\n### 3. Root Cause: Lack of QoS/Priority Scheduling in ScannerEngine\n\nThe architectural bottleneck is located in `/app/project/src/services/scanner_svc/scanner_engine.cpp`.\n\n**Key Findings:**\n\n1. **Single FIFO Queue**: The `ScannerEngine` uses a simple `std::queue<T>` wrapped in a `BlockingQueue` class. This is a strict First-In-First-Out (FIFO) data structure with **no priority support**.\n\n2. **No Tenant Differentiation**: When `submit(const ScanCommand &cmd)` is called, all scan commands - regardless of whether they come from Continuous Scan tenants or PAYG tenants - go into the same queue.\n\n3. **Queue Saturation During Peak Hours**: During peak business hours, Continuous Scan tenants generate a constant, high-volume stream of background scan requests. These fill up the queue (capacity: 1024 items) and occupy worker threads.\n\n4. **Head-of-Line Blocking**: When a PAYG tenant submits an on-demand scan, their request is placed at the **back of the queue**, behind potentially hundreds of Continuous Scan requests. The PAYG user must wait for all preceding requests to complete.\n\n5. **Follow-Up Never Implemented**: ADR-002 explicitly mentions \"Define **tenant partition-key strategy** for QoS isolation\" as a follow-up item. This was never implemented.\n\n**Ironic Design Flaw:**\nThe `PayGScanStrategy` class actually implements its own internal priority queue with support for `ScanPriority::High` and `ScanPriority::Normal` levels. However, this priority is **lost** when the work is submitted to the shared `ScannerEngine` - the engine's queue treats all items equally.\n\n### 4. Why This Causes Latency for PAYG Tenants\n\n**Scenario: Peak Hours**\n1. Multiple Continuous Scan tenants have active subscriptions\n2. Each generates scans every 1-30 seconds (configurable cadence)\n3. With 10 such tenants, each at 5-second intervals, that's ~120 background scans/minute\n4. Worker pool has `std::thread::hardware_concurrency()` workers (typically 4-16)\n5. Deep scans take 1-3 seconds each (per `scanner_engine.cpp` simulation)\n6. Queue fills with Continuous Scan jobs\n\n**Impact on PAYG User:**\n1. PAYG user requests an on-demand scan\n2. Request is queued behind 100+ Continuous Scan jobs\n3. With 8 workers processing 2-second jobs: wait time = (100/8) * 2s = **25 seconds minimum**\n4. Under heavier load, this can exceed minutes\n5. User perceives \"unacceptable latency\"\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\n### Primary Finding\n\n**The root cause of latency for Pay-As-You-Go tenants is the lack of Quality of Service (QoS) or priority-based scheduling in the `ScannerEngine`'s work queue.** The scanner engine uses a single, simple FIFO `BlockingQueue` that processes all scan requests in arrival order, regardless of tenant subscription type, request urgency, or business priority.\n\n### Key Components Involved\n\n1. **API Gateway** (`/app/project/src/services/api_gateway/routes.cpp`) - Request entry point\n2. **Command Handler** (`/app/project/src/lib/orchestration/command_handler.cpp`) - Command orchestration\n3. **Event Bus** (`/app/project/src/lib/infrastructure/event_bus/`) - Asynchronous message delivery\n4. **Scanner Service** (`/app/project/src/services/scanner_svc/service_impl.cpp`) - gRPC service facade\n5. **Scanner Engine** (`/app/project/src/services/scanner_svc/scanner_engine.cpp`) - **THE BOTTLENECK**\n\n### Architectural Pattern\n\nThe system correctly implements:\n- **Strategy Pattern** for pluggable scan policies per tenant type\n- **Command Pattern** for encapsulating user intents\n- **Chain-of-Responsibility** for validation pipelines\n- **Observer Pattern** for metrics and audit\n- **Event-Driven Architecture** for decoupled workflows\n\nHowever, it **fails to implement**:\n- **Priority Queue** or weighted fair scheduling in the execution layer\n- **Tenant isolation** at the worker pool level\n- **QoS guarantees** as noted in ADR-002 follow-ups\n\n### Recommendations\n\n1. **Implement Priority Queue**: Replace `std::queue` in `BlockingQueue` with `std::priority_queue` and add a priority field to `ScanCommand` that reflects tenant subscription type.\n\n2. **Tenant-Based Worker Partitioning**: Create separate worker pools for different SLA tiers (e.g., dedicated workers for PAYG tenants).\n\n3. **Implement Weighted Fair Queuing**: Allocate worker time proportionally based on tenant subscription value.\n\n4. **Add Queue Bypass for High-Priority**: Allow PAYG scans to bypass the standard queue during congestion.\n\n5. **Kafka Topic Partitioning**: Use separate Kafka partitions for different tenant tiers, with dedicated consumer groups.\n\nThe fundamental issue is an **architectural design flaw** where tenant-level differentiation implemented at the Strategy pattern level is **not propagated** to the shared execution infrastructure.\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":286,"cache_read_input_tokens":117022,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":286,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8099bb50-f87a-461b-a76d-e4d5ca0c0806","uuid":"6f86589b-8946-4195-bbd8-77a0154a824d"}

implementation with Chain-of-Responsibility validation pipeline |\n| `/app/project/src/lib/orchestration/command_handler.cpp` | Command handler that dispatches commands to appropriate executors via Strategy pattern |\n| `/app/project/src/lib/orchestration/command_handler.h` | Command handler interface with middleware pipeline and executor registration |\n| `/app/project/src/lib/domain/commands/scan_command.h` | ScanCommand domain object that encapsulates scan request intent and publishes to EventBus |\n| `/app/project/src/lib/domain/strategies/payg_scan_strategy.h` | Pay-As-You-Go strategy with its own priority queue and worker pool |\n| documenting the Event-Driven Architecture decisions |\n| `/app/project/docs/architecture/adr/003-grpc-and-service-mesh.md` | ADR documenting gRPC and Istio service mesh decisions |\n\n---\n\n## Code Evidence\n\n### 1. API Gateway Entry Point - Scan Request Initiation\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/services/api_gateway/routes.cpp\n// Lines: 209-239\n\n/**\n * POST /v1/security/scan\n */\nvoid initiateScan(const Http::Request& req,\n                  Http::ResponseWriter resp,\n                  const std::string& corrId)\n{\n    const auto tenantId = ensureAuthenticated(req);\n\n    rateLimiter_.acquireToken(tenantId, \"security.scan\");\n\n    json payload = parseJson(req);\n    validators::RequestValidator::validateScanRequest(payload);\n\n    auto dispatchResult = bus_->publish(\"InitiateSecurityScan\", {\n        { \"correlationId\", corrId },\n        { \"tenantId\",      tenantId },\n        { \"scanDepth\",     payload.at(\"scanDepth\") },\n        { \"targets\",       payload.at(\"targets\") }\n    });\n\n    if (!dispatchResult) {\n        throw errors::HttpError(Http::Code::Service_Unavailable,\n                                \"Unable to queue security scan at this time\");\n    }\n\n    resp.send(Http::Code::Accepted, json{\n        { \"message\", \"Security scan queued\" },\n        { \"correlationId\", corrId }\n    }.dump());\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Command Handler Orchestration Layer\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/lib/orchestration/command_handler.cpp\n// Lines: 178-217\n\nclass CommandHandler final\n{\npublic:\n    explicit CommandHandler(std::shared_ptr<CommandRegistry> registry)\n        : registry_{ std::move(registry) }\n    {\n        if (!registry_) { throw std::invalid_argument(\"registry is nullptr\"); }\n    }\n\n    // Synchronous (blocking) processing\n    void handle(const CommandContext& ctx, const Command& cmd)\n    {\n        notify_start(ctx, cmd);\n        try\n        {\n            run_validation(ctx, cmd);\n            run_execution(ctx, cmd);\n            notify_success(ctx, cmd);\n        }\n        catch (const std::exception& ex)\n        {\n            notify_error(ctx, cmd, ex);\n            throw;      // Re-propagate to upstream orchestrator\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Asynchronous (detached) processing — returns a future\n    std::future<void> dispatch_async(CommandContext ctx, Command cmd)\n    {\n        return std::async(std::launch::async,\n                          [self = shared_from_this(), ctx = std::move(ctx), cmd = std::move(cmd)]() {\n                              self->handle(ctx, cmd);\n                          });\n    }\n```\n\n### 3. **CRITICAL BOTTLENECK**: Scanner Engine with Single FIFO Queue\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/services/scanner_svc/scanner_engine.cpp\n// Lines: 200-241\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Thread-safe Queue (SIMPLE FIFO - NO PRIORITY)\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\ntemplate <typename T>\nclass BlockingQueue\n{\npublic:\n    explicit BlockingQueue(std::size_t max) : capacity_(max) {}\n\n    void push(T item)\n    {\n        std::unique_lock lk(mu_);\n        cvFull_.wait(lk, [&] { return queue_.size() < capacity_; });\n        queue_.push(std::move(item));  // FIFO: items processed in arrival order\n        cvEmpty_.notify_one();\n    }\n\n    bool pop(T &out)\n    {\n        std::unique_lock lk(mu_);\n        cvEmpty_.wait(lk, [&] { return !queue_.empty() || terminated_; });\n        if (queue_.empty()) return false; // terminated\n        out = std::move(queue_.front());\n        queue_.pop();\n        cvFull_.notify_one();\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    // ...\nprivate:\n    std::size_t                capacity_;\n    std::queue<T>              queue_;  // std::queue = FIFO, NO PRIORITY\n    std::mutex                 mu_;\n    std::condition_variable    cvFull_, cvEmpty_;\n    bool                       terminated_ {false};\n};\n```\n\n### 4. Scanner Engine Worker Pool - Shared Across All Tenants\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/services/scanner_svc/scanner_engine.cpp\n// Lines: 246-274\n\nclass ScannerEngine\n{\npublic:\n    explicit ScannerEngine(std::size_t workerCount = std::thread::hardware_concurrency())\n        : queue_(1024), log_(), shuttingDown_(false)  // Single queue with fixed capacity\n    {\n        workerCount = std::max<std::size_t>(1, workerCount);\n        log_.log(LogLevel::kInfo, \"Initializing ScannerEngine with \", workerCount, \" workers.\");\n\n        for (std::size_t i = 0; i < workerCount; ++i)\n        {\n            workers_.emplace_back([this, i] { workerLoop(i); });  // All workers share ONE queue\n        }\n    }\n\n    void submit(const ScanCommand &cmd)\n    {\n        if (shuttingDown_.load()) throw std::runtime_error(\"Engine is shutting down\");\n        queue_.push(cmd);  // ALL scan commands go to the SAME queue regardless of tenant type\n    }\n\n    // ...\nprivate:\n    BlockingQueue<ScanCommand>                  queue_;  // SINGLE shared queue for ALL tenants\n    std::vector<std::thread>                    workers_;\n    // ...\n};\n```\n\n### 5. Pay-As-You-Go Strategy - Has Its Own Priority Queue (But Not Used at Engine Level)\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/lib/domain/strategies/payg_scan_strategy.h\n// Lines: 220-236\n\nstruct QueueItem\n{\n    std::string correlationId;\n    ScanRequest request;\n\n    /* Highest priority first – normal > high => comparator reversed. */\n    bool operator<(const QueueItem& rhs) const noexcept\n    {\n        return static_cast<int>(request.priority) <\n               static_cast<int>(rhs.request.priority);\n    }\n};\n\n// ...\n/* Pending queue (priority). */\nstd::priority_queue<QueueItem> pending_;  // Strategy has priority queue internally\n```\n\n### 6. Continuous Scan Strategy - Background Worker Thread Generates Constant Load\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/lib/domain/strategies/continuous_scan_strategy.h\n// Lines: 186-199\n\n// Internal run-loop --------------------------------------------------------\nvoid runLoop()\n{\n    while (running_)\n    {\n        const auto now = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();\n        executeOneScanSafely();  // Generates continuous scan requests\n\n        std::unique_lock<std::mutex> lk(cvMutex_);\n        // Wait until either stop() is invoked or the cadence elapses.\n        cv_.wait_until(lk, now + cadence_, [this] { return !running_; });\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 7. Event Flow Documentation - Shows All Events Go Through Same Bus\n\n```markdown\n// File: /app/project/docs/architecture/event_flows.md\n// Lines: 13-38\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    autonumber\n    actor Client as Tenant / API Consumer\n    participant API as Presentation & API\n    participant ORCH as Orchestration\n    participant BUS as Event-Bus\n    participant SCN as ScannerSvc (Domain)\n\n    Client->>API: POST /v1/scan\\n{asset: \"web-1\", tier:\"deep\"}\n    API->>ORCH: Cmd<InitiateSecurityScan>\n    ORCH->>BUS: Publish(Cmd<InitiateSecurityScan>)\n    %% Broadcast to interested domain services\n    BUS-->>SCN: Cmd<InitiateSecurityScan>\n    SCN->>SCN: Validate + Enrich\n    // ALL scan commands go to the SAME ScannerSvc regardless of tenant type\n```\n\n### 8. ADR-002 - Event-Driven Architecture with Multi-Tenant QoS Note\n\n```markdown\n// File: /app/project/docs/architecture/adr/002-event-driven-architecture.md\n// Lines: 58-61\n\n### Follow-ups\n* Implement **Schema Registry** (Apache Avro) + compatibility gates in CI.\n* Define **tenant partition-key strategy** for QoS isolation.  // <-- THIS WAS NEVER IMPLEMENTED\n* Provide **SDKs** (Go, Rust, C++) with consistent observability hooks.\n```\n\n---\n\n## Analysis\n\n### 1. End-to-End Request Lifecycle for On-Demand Security Scans\n\nThe lifecycle of an on-demand security scan request follows this path:\n\n```\n[Client]\n    → POST /v1/security/scan (HTTP/REST)\n    → [API Gateway] routes.cpp:initiateScan()\n        → Authentication via JWT/mTLS\n        → Rate limiting per tenant\n        → JSON validation\n    → [Event Bus] Publish \"InitiateSecurityScan\" event\n    → [Orchestration Layer] command_handler.cpp\n        → Chain-of-Responsibility validation (TenantQuotaValidator, ComplianceValidator)\n        → Strategy resolution based on command type\n    → [Event Consumer] event_consumer.cpp\n        → Kafka consumer dispatches to domain handlers\n    → [Scanner Service] service_impl.cpp:InitiateScan()\n        → Acquires scan strategy from registry\n        → Publishes ScanStarted domain event\n        → Enqueues work to ScannerEngine\n    → [Scanner Engine] scanner_engine.cpp\n        → Work item placed in BlockingQueue\n        → Worker thread picks up and executes scan\n    → [Event Bus] Publish scan completion event\n```\n\n### 2. Architectural Interactions Between Tenant Types\n\nThe system supports different subscription plans with distinct scan behaviors:\n\n**Continuous Scan Tenants:**\n- Have a `ContinuousScanStrategy` that runs a background thread\n- Generates scan requests at a configurable cadence (minimum 1 second)\n- Produces a **constant stream** of scan commands regardless of user activity\n- These scans are typically automated, running 24/7\n\n**Pay-As-You-Go (PAYG) Tenants:**\n- Use `PayGScanStrategy` triggered by explicit user requests\n- On-demand, sporadic scan requests\n- Users expect immediate responsiveness\n- Billed per-scan, so latency directly impacts customer experience\n\n**The Critical Interaction:**\nBoth tenant types' scan requests ultimately flow through the **same ScannerEngine** with its **single shared FIFO BlockingQueue**. The architectural separation at the Strategy level does NOT extend to the execution layer.\n\n### 3. Root Cause: Lack of QoS/Priority Scheduling in ScannerEngine\n\nThe architectural bottleneck is located in `/app/project/src/services/scanner_svc/scanner_engine.cpp`.\n\n**Key Findings:**\n\n1. **Single FIFO Queue**: The `ScannerEngine` uses a simple `std::queue<T>` wrapped in a `BlockingQueue` class. This is a strict First-In-First-Out (FIFO) data structure with **no priority support**.\n\n2. **No Tenant Differentiation**: When `submit(const ScanCommand &cmd)` is called, all scan commands - regardless of whether they come from Continuous Scan tenants or PAYG tenants - go into the same queue.\n\n3. **Queue Saturation During Peak Hours**: During peak business hours, Continuous Scan tenants generate a constant, high-volume stream of background scan requests. These fill up the queue (capacity: 1024 items) and occupy worker threads.\n\n4. **Head-of-Line Blocking**: When a PAYG tenant submits an on-demand scan, their request is placed at the **back of the queue**, behind potentially hundreds of Continuous Scan requests. The PAYG user must wait for all preceding requests to complete.\n\n5. **Follow-Up Never Implemented**: ADR-002 explicitly mentions \"Define **tenant partition-key strategy** for QoS isolation\" as a follow-up item. This was never implemented.\n\n**Ironic Design Flaw:**\nThe `PayGScanStrategy` class actually implements its own internal priority queue with support for `ScanPriority::High` and `ScanPriority::Normal` levels. However, this priority is **lost** when the work is submitted to the shared `ScannerEngine` - the engine's queue treats all items equally.\n\n### 4. Why This Causes Latency for PAYG Tenants\n\n**Scenario: Peak Hours**\n1. Multiple Continuous Scan tenants have active subscriptions\n2. Each generates scans every 1-30 seconds (configurable cadence)\n3. With 10 such tenants, each at 5-second intervals, that's ~120 background scans/minute\n4. Worker pool has `std::thread::hardware_concurrency()` workers (typically 4-16)\n5. Deep scans take 1-3 seconds each (per `scanner_engine.cpp` simulation)\n6. Queue fills with Continuous Scan jobs\n\n**Impact on PAYG User:**\n1. PAYG user requests an on-demand scan\n2. Request is queued behind 100+ Continuous Scan jobs\n3. With 8 workers processing 2-second jobs: wait time = (100/8) * 2s = **25 seconds minimum**\n4. Under heavier load, this can exceed minutes\n5. User perceives \"unacceptable latency\"\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\n### Primary Finding\n\n**The root cause of latency for Pay-As-You-Go tenants is the lack of Quality of Service (QoS) or priority-based scheduling in the `ScannerEngine`'s work queue.** The scanner engine uses a single, simple FIFO `BlockingQueue` that processes all scan requests in arrival order, regardless of tenant subscription type, request urgency, or business priority.\n\n### Key Components Involved\n\n1. **API Gateway** (`/app/project/src/services/api_gateway/routes.cpp`) - Request entry point\n2. **Command Handler** (`/app/project/src/lib/orchestration/command_handler.cpp`) - Command orchestration\n3. **Event Bus** (`/app/project/src/lib/infrastructure/event_bus/`) - Asynchronous message delivery\n4. **Scanner Service** (`/app/project/src/services/scanner_svc/service_impl.cpp`) - gRPC service facade\n5. **Scanner Engine** (`/app/project/src/services/scanner_svc/scanner_engine.cpp`) - **THE BOTTLENECK**\n\n### Architectural Pattern\n\nThe system correctly implements:\n- **Strategy Pattern** for pluggable scan policies per tenant type\n- **Command Pattern** for encapsulating user intents\n- **Chain-of-Responsibility** for validation pipelines\n- **Observer Pattern** for metrics and audit\n- **Event-Driven Architecture** for decoupled workflows\n\nHowever, it **fails to implement**:\n- **Priority Queue** or weighted fair scheduling in the execution layer\n- **Tenant isolation** at the worker pool level\n- **QoS guarantees** as noted in ADR-002 follow-ups\n\n### Recommendations\n\n1. **Implement Priority Queue**: Replace `std::queue` in `BlockingQueue` with `std::priority_queue` and add a priority field to `ScanCommand` that reflects tenant subscription type.\n\n2. **Tenant-Based Worker Partitioning**: Create separate worker pools for different SLA tiers (e.g., dedicated workers for PAYG tenants).\n\n3. **Implement Weighted Fair Queuing**: Allocate worker time proportionally based on tenant subscription value.\n\n4. **Add Queue Bypass for High-Priority**: Allow PAYG scans to bypass the standard queue during congestion.\n\n5. **Kafka Topic Partitioning**: Use separate Kafka partitions for different tenant tiers, with dedicated consumer groups.\n\nThe fundamental issue is an **architectural design flaw** where tenant-level differentiation implemented at the Strategy pattern level is **not propagated** to the shared execution infrastructure.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
